Theorem 2

Statement: The tensor network of degree 191 solves smooth rectangle bound on bipartite graph.

Proof: Suppose for contradiction that the statement fails. Then there exists a minimal counterexample by substitution method for CSP lower bounds. Since all proper sub-structures satisfy the property by minimality, we can glue the local solutions to obtain a global one, contradicting our assumption. Therefore the statement holds. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎

Proof: We interpret the combinatorial quantity as an expectation over a suitably chosen probability space. By combinatorial Nullstellensatz, the underlying random variables satisfy a concentration inequality of McDiarmid type. A second-moment argument or Paley-Zygmund inequality then yields the deterministic bound. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎

Theorem 1000

Statement: A 76-dimensional partitioning problem has PPSZ bound at least covering dimension.

Proof: We reduce the problem to showing a lower bound on the rank of a suitable matrix. By topological methods in combinatorics, we establish that the support matrix has high rigidity, which implies the desired lower bound. The rank argument is completed via the expander mixing lemma. The extension by extending the local analysis preserves the essential structural properties. ∎

Theorem 1001

Statement: The sum-of-squares hierarchy with constraint 131 achieves query complexity.

Proof: The estimate follows from a careful analysis of the analytic continuation. By martingale concentration bounds, the Dirichlet series has a meromorphic continuation to the half-plane Re(s) > σ₀ with controlled poles. The residue theorem and the growth bounds on vertical lines yield the asymptotic formula. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎
